OPINION: This is a technically solid, well-written thesis with a carefully designed hybrid model and extensive experiments, but its reliance on synthetic primary data limits it for a top-tier journal.

2. PARSED COMPONENTS (Stage 0)  

- PROBLEM:  
  The thesis addresses multi-horizon prediction of passenger boarding counts at bus stops in an urban bus network, with a primary focus on the Astana bus network. - MOTIVATION:  
  The authors argue that accurate real-time passenger flow prediction is critical for operational planning, dynamic dispatching, and crowd management in rapidly growing cities like Astana.   They highlight three limitations of existing work: (L1) graph-based methods using only convolutional message passing struggle with fine-grained temporal dynamics; (L2) most models assume a fixed physical graph and ignore latent spatial dependencies; and (L3) common deterministic or Gaussian outputs are ill-suited for overdispersed count data.   DTS-GSSF consists of: (i) a Gated Recurrent Encoder (GRE) that processes per-station time series with an input-dependent forget gate; (ii) a GraphPropagation layer with three message-passing hops over a combined adjacency \(\alpha A_{phys} + (1-\alpha)A_{adp}\); (iii) a TemporalAttention module (multi-head self-attention over the 72-hour context per station); and (iv) per-station and aggregate prediction heads producing Negative Binomial parameters, with LoRA used on selected projections for efficient adaptation. - SUMMARY/METHOD:  
  The proposed DTS-GSSF model combines a Gated Recurrent Encoder for temporal encoding, dual graph propagation over physical and learned adaptive adjacency matrices, and multi-head temporal self-attention per station, with Negative Binomial likelihood outputs for probabilistic forecasting. - SUMMARY/RESULTS:  
  On a synthetic Astana dataset with 1.3M station-hours and 374 stations, DTS-GSSF achieves \(R^2 = 0.978 \pm 0.001\) and MAE \(= 11.65 \pm 0.14\), outperforming ten baselines including HA, LSTM, GRU, TCN, STGCN, Graph WaveNet, and AGCRN, especially when hierarchical aggregates are included. - SUMMARY/LIMITATIONS:  
  The main limitation is that the primary dataset is synthetic, so real-world performance on Astana operator data remains to be validated, and some variance at bottom-level stations is likely irreducible given the available features. 4. CRITERION-WISE EVALUATION (Stage 2)  

### CRITERION: 1) Novelty & Originality  

- TEXT-BASED-EVIDENCE:  
  - The Introduction and positioning section (Sec. 1, Sec. 3.5) claim that DTS-GSSF is the first model to integrate a gated recurrent encoder, dual physical/adaptive adjacency, multi-head temporal attention, and Negative Binomial likelihood in a compact architecture targeting real-time bus passenger flow prediction. - EXTERNAL-KNOWLEDGE:  
  - EXTERNAL-KNOWLEDGE: Hybrid spatiotemporal GNN architectures and NB likelihoods for demand/count forecasting have been explored in various works beyond those cited here; combining them is often seen as an incremental advance rather than fundamentally new theory.  
  - EXTERNAL-KNOWLEDGE: LoRA-style parameter-efficient fine-tuning is widely used in NLP and vision; its application to graph/time-series forecasting is emerging but not unprecedented.  

- STRENGTHS:  
  - OPINION: The combination of GRE + dual adjacency + temporal attention + NB head is a coherent architectural package with clear motivation from the limitations (L1–L3) identified in the Introduction and literature review. ### CRITERION: 4) Empirical Results & Analysis  

- TEXT-BASED-EVIDENCE:  
  - Main results tables (5.1 and 5.2) show clear, substantial gains in \(R^2\), MAE, RMSE, and MAPE for DTS-GSSF over baselines on both synthetic and LACMTA datasets, with DTS-GSSF outperforming AGCRN by a notable margin. - TEXT-BASED-EVIDENCE:

6. FINAL RECOMMENDATION (Stage 4)  

- OVERALL-SCORE-[0-10]: 7  
- OVERALL-CONFIDENCE-[0.0-1.0]: 0.84  

- RECOMMENDATION: Weak Accept  

- ONE-PARAGRAPH-JUSTIFICATION:  
  OPINION: This thesis presents a well-motivated, technically sound hybrid spatiotemporal model (DTS-GSSF) for passenger flow prediction, with a clear problem formulation, detailed methodology, and an unusually comprehensive empirical evaluation including ablations, calibration, robustness, cross-city generalization, and system integration.  The main weakness, especially for a Q1 journal, is that the richest set of experiments and analyses is performed on a synthetic dataset, with only one real-world open dataset used for validation and no reported deployments with transit agencies.  Nevertheless, the clarity of exposition, the solid engineering of the architecture, and the strong performance on both synthetic and LACMTA data justify a positive but cautious recommendation, particularly if the work is framed as an applied systems paper rather than purely methodological.
